<meta>This page continues the third chapter of the Haṭhayogapradīpikā, moving from the description of the three locks (Bandhas) to the specific practice of Viparītakaraṇī Mudrā. It builds on the previous page's warning that the "Sun" (at the navel) consumes the "Moon’s nectar" (at the palate), leading to old age.</meta>

( 106 )
### The Light on Haṭha Yoga

**Root Verse: There exists a divine practice that deceives the mouth of the Sun. It must be known through the instruction of a Guru, and not through even millions of scriptural explanations. || 78 ||**

**Root Verse: When the navel is above and the palate is below, the Sun is above and the Moon is below. This is known as the "Inverted Action," obtained only through the words of a Guru. || 79 ||**

**Root Verse: For the practitioner who is engaged in daily practice, the digestive fire is greatly increased. Therefore, that practitioner should ensure they consume a large amount of food. || 80 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

**Regarding the phrase "There exists" (tatra):** In this context, there is a **divine**—meaning excellent—**practice** (karaṇam), which will be described as a "seal" <note>term: <term>Mudrā</term> — a symbolic or ritual gesture or posture in yoga used to direct energy</note>. By this practice, the **mouth of the Sun**, which is located at the navel, is **deceived** or bypassed. This **must be known through the instruction of a Guru**; it is only possible to understand it through a teacher's guidance. It is **not** possible to understand it **through even millions of scriptural explanations**. || 78 ||

Now, the author describes the **Inverted Action** <note>term: <term>Viparītakaraṇī</term> — literally "inverted (viparīta) doing (karaṇī)"; a practice where the body is turned upside down</note>. **When the navel is above:** This refers to the Yogi for whom the navel is in the upper position, and the **palate**—the region of the palate—is in the lower position. In such a Yogi, the **Sun**, which is of the nature of burning, is **above** in the upper part, and the **Moon**, which is of the nature of nectar <note>original: *amṛtātmā* — "soul of nectar"</note>, is **below** in the lower part. According to an alternative reading: when the Yogi becomes one with the navel above and the palate below, then the Sun is above and the Moon is below. This interpretation follows the logical connection of "when... then." This practice is called **Inverted** because it reverses the usual positions of the Moon and Sun <note>In the standard physical model of Hatha Yoga, the Moon resides in the head and the Sun in the stomach; the inversion physically flips this hierarchy</note>. It is **obtained**, meaning attained, **only through the words of a Guru**, and not by any other means. || 79 ||

**Regarding the phrase "Daily" (nityam):** For one who is **engaged** and attentive in **daily**—everyday—**practice**, the **digestive fire** <note>term: <term>Jaṭharāgni</term> — the "belly-fire" responsible for digestion</note> is greatly increased. For such a **practitioner** <note>original: *sādhaka*</note>, a **large amount of food must be provided**. || 80 ||

->॥ VERNACULAR COMMENTARY ॥<-

**Regarding "There exists" (tatra):** In this chapter, one should know how to deceive the mouth of the **Sun**, which is the form of fire located in the navel. Such a **divine** and excellent **practice** (mudrā), which we will call the "Inverted Action Seal," is worthy of being known through the **instruction of Gurus**. It cannot be understood even through the meanings of millions of scriptures. || 78 ||

Now the **Inverted Action Seal** is described. **When the navel is above:** For a Yogi who has the navel in the upper part and the palate-region in the lower part, the **Sun** in the form of burning heat resides in the upper part, and the **Moon** in the form of nectar resides in the lower part. This is the practice named **Inverted**. The Moon is usually above and the Sun below; making the Sun above and the Moon below is achieved through the **words of Gurus**, and occurs in no other way. || 79 ||

**Regarding "Daily" (nityam):** For one who practices this daily, the **Inverted Action** increases the **digestive fire**.

<meta>catchword: vip- (Inverted)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces Viparītakaraṇī (the Inverted Action), a practice that physically reverses the body's orientation to prevent the "Sun" in the navel from consuming the "Moon's nectar" from the head. It emphasizes that this secret technique requires a Guru's guidance and results in a significantly increased appetite due to heightened digestive fire.</summary>
<keywords>Viparītakaraṇī, Inverted Action, Mudrā, Sun and Moon, Jaṭharāgni, digestive fire, Guru's instruction, Haṭha Yoga, nectar, yoga diet</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Viparītakaraṇī Mudrā (the Inverted Action) in the third chapter of the Haṭhayogapradīpikā. It specifically addresses the dietary requirements for this practice and provides the physical technique and long-term benefits of the posture, such as reversing the signs of aging.</meta>

( 107 )
### Chapter Three

**Root Verse: If the food intake is sparse, the fire burns [the body] instantly. On the first day, one should remain with the head down and feet upwards for only a moment. || 81 ||**

**Root Verse: One should increase the practice slightly day by day. After six months, wrinkles and grey hair are no longer seen. He who practices this daily for even one *yāma* <note>term: <term>yāma</term> — a traditional unit of time lasting approximately three hours</note> becomes a conqueror of time. || 82 ||**

### ->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

...**the fire**—referring to the abdominal fire—**is an increaser.** Specifically, "increaser" describes the Inverted Action (<note>original: <term>viparītakaraṇī</term></note>), as it greatly stimulates this fire. For that practitioner who practices the Inverted Action, the **diet**—the food consumed—should be **copious** and prepared according to their desire. The particle "ca" <note>original: <term>च</term></note> is used here merely to complete the verse's meter. || 80 ||

**Regarding the phrase "If the food intake is sparse" (alpāhāraḥ):** If one becomes an **eater of little food**—meaning one whose consumption of desired foods is minimal—then at that time, the **fire**—the gastric fire (<note>original: <term>jaṭharānala</term></note>)—would **burn** the body in just a moment. This means it would consume the tissues quickly. 

Now, the author explains the physical action of placing the Sun and Moon (the navel and palate) in their opposite positions: **"Head down..."** He who has his **head down** in the lower part, on the ground, is "head-down" (<note>original: <term>adhaḥ-śirāḥ</term></note>). Supporting the waist area with the hands, and bracing against the ground with the arms from the armpits to the elbows, along with the shoulders, the back of the neck, and the back of the head, one becomes "head-down." He who has his **feet upwards** in the space above is "feet-up" (<note>original: <term>ūrdhva-pādaḥ</term></note>). On the **first day**—the day of beginning—one should remain so for only a **moment**. || 81 ||

**Regarding the phrase "From a moment" (kṣaṇāt):** **Day by day**, meaning every day, one should **practice** by increasing the duration slightly beyond a moment—to two moments, then three—increasing it by one increment each day. 

Now, the author describes the benefits of the Inverted Action: **"Wrinkles..."** **Wrinkles** (<note>original: <term>valitaṃ</term></note>) refers to the sagging or folding of the skin, and **grey hair** (<note>original: <term>palitaṃ</term></note>) refers to the whitening of the hair. After the passage of **six months**, these are no longer seen.

### ->॥ VERNACULAR COMMENTARY ॥<-

...the **Inverted Action** (<note>original: <term>viparītakaraṇī</term></note>) is performed. For the person practicing this Inverted Action, it is proper to consume food very much according to their desire. || 80 ||

**Regarding sparse diet:** If the person performing the Inverted Action eats very little food, then the ignited gastric fire will quickly burn the body. Now the technique is described: placing the head on the earth and supporting the waist with both arms, one should remain with the legs situated upwards in the sky. On the first day of starting, remain for only a moment. || 81 ||

**Regarding the increase:** Then, day by day, increase the time slightly—on the second day two moments, on the third day three moments—thus practicing with daily increase. Now the benefits of the Inverted Action are stated: in the body of the one who does this, **wrinkles**—where the skin becomes folded—and **white hair** will not be seen after six months have passed. If...

It describes the physical posture (similar to a shoulder stand) and claims that consistent practice can reverse aging and eventually "conquer time."</summary>

हठयोगप्रदीपिकायां <note>original: "In the Hatha Yoga Pradipika"</note>

# Now, Vajrolī ||

**Root Verse: Even while living according to one's own will, without the rules prescribed in Yoga, he who knows Vajrolī is a yogi and a vessel of success. || 83 ||**

**Root Verse: I shall speak of two things there which are difficult for just anyone to obtain: the first is milk, and the second is a submissive woman. || 84 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

...is no longer seen; it is not observed. "On the body of the practitioner" is the implied phrase here. The practitioner who daily practices for a **yāma** <note>term: <term>yāma</term> — a traditional unit of time lasting approximately three hours</note>, which is one watch of the day, becomes a **conqueror of time** (<note>original: <term>kālajit</term></note>), meaning he triumphs over death. 

By this, it is also indicated that Yoga has the power to obstruct **prārabdha karma** <note>term: <term>prārabdha karma</term> — the portion of past karma that is responsible for the present body and life circumstances</note>. As stated in the *Vishnu Dharma*: "Even the karma that initiated one's own body can be exhausted. Listen, O King, to the characteristics of that Yoga." 

Vidyāraṇya <note>note: a famous 14th-century scholar and sage</note> also stated in the *Jīvanmukti-viveka*: "Just as prārabdha karma is stronger than the knowledge of the absolute truth, so too is the practice of Yoga stronger than even that karma." It is for this reason that the departure from the body at will by yogis such as Uddālaka and Vītahavya is possible. It is also stated in the *Bhāgavata*: "He left the body through the means of Yoga." || 82 ||

To generate interest in the practice of Vajrolī, the author first describes its fruits. **"According to one's own will..."**: The practitioner who **knows**—meaning he understands specifically through his own experience—the **Vajrolī Mudrā**, that yogi remains a **vessel of success** (<note>original: <term>siddhi-bhājanam</term></note>). He becomes a fit recipient for the **siddhis** <note>term: <term>siddhis</term> — supernatural powers such as <term>aṇimā</term> (the ability to become as small as an atom)</note>, even if he is **living according to his own will**—acting as he pleases—**without the rules** such as celibacy (<note>original: <term>brahmacharya</term></note>) mentioned in the scriptures of Yoga. || 83 ||

The author now describes two items useful as tools for that practice: **"In that..."**: In the practice of Vajrolī, there are two items...

->॥ VERNACULAR TRANSLATION ॥<-

By practicing the Inverted Action (<note>original: <term>viparītakaraṇī</term></note>) until one can sustain it for one *yāma* (three hours), that yogi becomes a conqueror of death. Through this, it is shown that Yoga removes the effects of *prārabdha karma*. Just as *prārabdha karma* is more powerful than the knowledge of reality, so too is the practice of Yoga more powerful than *prārabdha karma*. It is said that yogis like Uddālaka and Vītahavya abandoned their bodies by their own will. Therefore, Yoga is supreme. || 82 ||

Now, at the beginning of the section on Vajrolī, its fruits are told. **"According to one's own will..."**: That practitioner of yoga who specifically knows the Vajrolī Mudrā through his own experience, that yogi—even without following the rules of celibacy and such as mentioned in the Yoga scriptures, and while living according to his own desires—becomes a consumer of the eight *siddhis*, beginning with *aṇimā*. || 83 ||

**"In that..."**: In the practice of Vajrolī, two things are mentioned which are difficult for a poor man to obtain...

### Third Instruction ###

**Root Verse: One should gradually practice the proper upward contraction of the genitals. Whether a man or even a woman, one may attain success in Vajrolī. || 85 ||**

**Root Verse: With effort, using a proper tube, one should perform a blowing action into the "Vajra-cave" <note>original: "Vajrakandare" — referring to the urethral opening</note>. This should be done slowly and gradually to facilitate the circulation of air. || 86 ||**

---

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

**I shall speak** or describe. What are the two things which are difficult to obtain for just anyone, or for someone without means? They are either "hard to acquire" or "impossible to acquire." This is supported by the dictionary <note>original: *kośa*</note> definition that the prefix *duḥ* signifies difficulty or prohibition. 

In response to the question "What are those two things?", the text says: **Milk**. One thing is milk for drinking. After using the organ, because of the resulting weakness of the senses, drinking milk is appropriate to regain strength. Some practitioners say milk is for suction <note>original: *ākarṣaṇa*</note> during the practice itself, but that is incorrect; if the milk were to thicken or curdle inside, it would be impossible to expel. The second thing is a woman who is submissive and under one's own control. || 84 ||

The method of **Vajrolī Mudrā** <note>term: <term>Vajrolī Mudrā</term> — an advanced Hatha Yoga practice involving the control and sublimation of sexual energy and fluids</note> is now described. **By the organ...** Following union with a woman, using the discharge of the **Bindu** <note>term: <term>Bindu</term> — literally "drop," referring to the vital seed or semen, considered the essence of life force</note> as the means of the practice, a man or even a woman should practice the gradual and correct upward contraction through effort. By contracting the genitals and drawing the seed upward, one may attain success in Vajrolī Mudrā. || 85 ||

Now the preliminary process of Vajrolī is described. **With effort...** With a "proper tube," meaning a tube made of lead <note>original: *sīsaka*</note> or similar materials, one should perform a blowing action <note>original: *phutkāra*</note> slowly and gradually, much like one blows through a pipe to kindle a fire. This blowing into the **Vajra-cave** (<note>original: <term>Vajrakandara</term></note>), the opening of the urethra, is done to ensure the circulation of air moves properly through the channel. Therefore, one should perform this repeatedly and with great care. Now, regarding the means of practicing Vajrolī...

---

->॥ VERNACULAR EXPOSITION ॥<-

First, one should drink milk because after union with a woman, the senses become weak; therefore, drinking milk is necessary. The second thing is an obedient and submissive woman. || 84 ||

Now the method of Vajrolī Mudrā is stated. **By the organ...** After union with a woman, when the "drop" falls, the man or even the woman should practice contracting the organ upward with effort. By pulling the vital seed back up, success in Vajrolī Mudrā is obtained. || 85 ||

Now the preliminary action of Vajrolī is stated. **With effort...** Using a tube made of silver <note>original: *chāndi*</note>, one should blow into the opening of the organ slowly, just as one blows to light a fire, so that air circulates. This should be done repeatedly. Now the process of practice is stated: a probe <note>original: *śalākā*</note> should be made of lead, four finger-widths long, which is smooth and suitable for entering the organ...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Vajrolī Mudrā, specifically detailing the physical preparations involving catheterization and the goal of fluid retention (bindu-dhāraṇa). ( 110 )
**In the Haṭhayogapradīpikā**

# **Root: Through practice, one should draw the vital fluid falling into the woman's vagina upwards.**
# **Even if one's own vital fluid has been discharged, one should draw it upwards and preserve it. || 87 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

**Action.** Having commissioned a smooth probe <note>original: *śalākā* — a rod or catheter</note> made of lead <note>original: *sīsaka* — historically used for medical instruments, though toxic by modern standards</note>, suitable for insertion into the male organ <note>original: *meḍhra*</note> and measuring fourteen finger-widths <note>term: <term>aṅgula</term> — a traditional unit of measurement roughly equal to the width of a finger</note> in length, one should practice its insertion into the organ. On the first day, one should insert it to the depth of one finger-width. On the second day, two finger-widths. On the third day, three finger-widths. In this gradual manner, when the insertion reaches twelve finger-widths, the urethral passage becomes purified. 

Then, one should have a similar probe made, fourteen finger-widths long, but with a one-finger-width curve at the tip, facing upwards. One should insert this twelve finger-widths deep, leaving the two-finger-width curved, upward-facing portion outside. Then, taking a tube similar to a goldsmith's blowpipe <note>original: *agnidhamana-sādhani-bhūta-nāla*</note>, one should insert its tip into the curved, upward-facing two-finger portion of the tube already inside the organ and perform a blowing action. By this, the passage is thoroughly purified. 

After that, one should practice drawing up water through the male organ. Once the drawing of water is mastered, one should practice drawing the vital fluid <note>term: <term>bindu</term> — literally "drop" or "point," referring here to the essence of life-force or semen</note> upwards according to the method described in the previous verse. When the drawing up of the vital fluid is perfected, the success of **Vajrolī Mudrā** is achieved. This is only accomplished by one who has conquered their breath <note>original: *jitaprāṇa*</note> and no one else. It becomes fully effective when both **Khecarī Mudrā** <note>term: <term>Khecarī Mudrā</term> — a practice involving the tongue being turned back into the nasal cavity</note> and the mastery of breath are attained. || 86 ||

As such, when the practice of Vajrolī is mastered, the author describes the subsequent practice: **In the woman's vagina...**

->॥ VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ॥<-

Having done this, one should practice entering it into the organ. On the first day, insert it one finger-width. On the second day, insert two finger-widths. On the third day, insert three finger-widths. In this gradual way, when it reaches twelve finger-widths, the passage of the organ is purified. That fourteen-finger probe should be made such that two finger-widths are curved with the opening facing upwards. Then, insert twelve finger-widths inside and keep the two-finger-width curved, upward-facing opening on the outside. After that, take a tube like the one a goldsmith uses to blow on a fire. Place the front of that tube into the middle of the two-finger-width curved opening of the probe that was inserted into the organ, and then blow. By doing this, the passage of the organ is thoroughly purified. 

Following this, one should practice pulling water upwards through the organ. When the attraction <note>original: *ākarṣaṇa* — suction or drawing in</note> of water begins to happen properly, then, using the method described in the previous verse, one should practice the attraction of the vital fluid. When the attraction and drawing in of the vital fluid is mastered, then **Vajrolī Mudrā** is perfected. To whomever **Khecarī Mudrā** and the conquest of breath are both mastered, to that person **Vajrolī Mudrā** will be successful, and not to anyone else. || 86 ||

When the practice of **Vajrolī Mudrā** is thus perfected, the further means are described: **In the woman...**

<vocab>Hathayogapradipika, Vajroli Mudra, Bindu, Pranajaya, Khechari Mudra, Shalaka, yoga practice</vocab>

<summary>This page provides highly technical and physical instructions for the preparation of Vajrolī Mudrā, involving the gradual use of a lead catheter and blowpipe to purify the urethral passage. The ultimate goal is the yogic ability to draw fluids—first water, then vital seed—upwards to achieve physical and spiritual preservation.</summary>

**Third Instruction**

> **Root: Thus, the knower of Yoga should preserve the vital essence; in doing so, he conquers death. Death comes from the loss of the essence, and life comes from its retention. || 88 ||**
> **A sweet fragrance is produced in the yogi's body from the retention of this vital essence. As long as the essence remains steady in the body, why should there be any fear of death? || 89 ||**

## ॥ COMMENTARY ॥

That which is falling is called "the falling fluid" <note>original: *patad-bindu*</note>. At the time of sexual union, one should, through the practice of **Vajrolī Mudrā**, draw that falling essence <note>term: <term>bindu</term> — often translated as "drop" or "seed," it refers to the vital masculine energy or semen</term> upwards before it actually falls. If the essence is not drawn up before it falls, then one should draw up that which has already fallen, as the text says: *And the displaced...* This refers to one’s own essence that has been discharged into the woman's anatomy; one should draw it back up along with the female essence <note>term: <term>rajas</term> — the female vital energy, often associated with menstrual fluid or vaginal secretions</note> and establish it within. || 87 || <note>Note: The commentary here references the verse numbered 87 on the previous page.</note>

Now the author describes the qualities of **Vajrolī**: *Thus...* He who preserves the vital essence in the manner described is a "knower of Yoga" <note>original: *yogavit*</note> and overcomes death. Because death occurs through the falling or loss of the fluid, retention of the essence results in life. Therefore, the meaning is that one must protect and preserve this essence. || 88 ||

*A sweet fragrance...* In the body of a yogi practicing **Vajrolī**, a beautiful and pleasant scent arises from the retention of the vital fluid. As long as the essence is steady in the body, how can there be fear of death? It means there is no fear from any source. || 89 ||

## ॥ VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ॥

*In the womb...* <note>original: *bhage* — referring here to the female reproductive organ</note> At the time of union, if the vital seed <note>original: *vīrya*</note> is about to fall into the woman's vagina, one should first draw that seed upward through the practice of **Vajrolī**. If the drawing up does not happen before the fall, and the seed has already fallen into the woman's vagina, then one should pull one's own seed and the woman's essence <note>original: *rajas*</note> back upward and establish it. || 87 ||

Now the benefits of **Vajrolī** are told: *Thus...* He who makes the vital essence steady in this way becomes a knower of Yoga. He conquers death. Through the falling of the seed, death occurs. By holding the seed in this manner, life is sustained. Therefore, one should keep the essence steady by this method. || 88 ||

*A sweet fragrance...* For the practitioner of **Vajrolī**, a very beautiful fragrance manifests in the body due to the retention of the seed. And as long as the essence remains steady, there is no fear of Time <note>original: *kāla* — a common synonym for Death</note>. || 89 ||

<summary>This page details the practice of Vajrolī Mudrā during sexual union, specifically the technique of drawing both male and female vital fluids upward to achieve physical immortality and a divine bodily fragrance.</summary>
<keywords>Vajroli Mudra, Bindu, Rajas, immortality, vital essence, Hatha Yoga, fluid retention, conquering death</keywords>

# In the Light on Haṭha Yoga

**Root: For men, the vital fluid is dependent on the mind, and life is dependent on the vital fluid. Therefore, the vital fluid and the mind must be protected with great effort. || 90 ||**
**In the same way, one should protect the female essence of a woman in her season, as well as one’s own fluid. The practitioner who knows the method of this exercise should draw it upward through the male organ. || 91 ||**
**Sahajolī and Amarolī are variations of Vajrolī. One should place fine ash, produced from burnt cow dung, into water... || 92 ||**

->## ॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

Regarding the verse *Dependent on the mind...*: Indeed, because for men, the <term>shukra</term> <note>original: *śukraṃ* — vital fluid or semen</note> is dependent on the mind; when the mind is restless, the fluid is restless, and when the mind is steady, the fluid is steady. Since life is dependent on the <term>shukra</term>—for as long as the fluid is steady, there is life, and when it is lost, there is death—life is under its control. Therefore, the <term>bindu</term> <note>original: *bindu* — the point or drop of vital essence</note> and the mind <note>original: *mānasam*</note> must be protected through superior effort. The word "indeed" <note>original: *eva*</note> emphasizes that this protection is absolutely necessary. || 90 ||

Regarding the verse *A woman in her season...*: Through the practice previously described, a woman who is "in her season" <note>original: *ṛtumatī* — a woman during or just after her menstrual period</note>—specifically one who has bathed after her period—possesses <term>rajas</term> <note>original: *retas* used here synonymously with female essence</note>. One should protect both her essence and one’s own fluid. He shows the previous practice again with the words *Through the organ...*: One who knows the union of practice <note>original: *abhyāsa-yoga-vit*</note>—meaning one who knows the practice of **Vajrolī** because it is a means to achieve Yoga—should draw it upward through the male organ <note>original: *meḍhreṇa* — the penis or urethra</note> with thorough effort. The objects "female essence" and "fluid" are implied here. <note>This verse is considered an interpolation [original: *kṣiptaḥ*] by the commentator, meaning it may have been added to the text later by a different author.</note> || 91 ||

Wishing to speak of **Sahajolī** and **Amarolī**, he explains their status as specific types of **Vajrolī**: *Sahajolī...* **Sahajolī** and **Amarolī** are distinctions or variations of **Vajrolī**. The reason for this is "on the one hand" <note>original: *ekataḥ*</note>, meaning they are essentially one or lead to the same result. Regarding **Sahajolī**: *In water...* Cow dung that has been burnt is called "burnt cow dung" <note>original: *dagdha-gomaya*</note>; that which is produced from it...

->## ॥ VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ॥<-

Regarding the verse *Dependent on the mind...*: Certainly, if the mind becomes restless, then the man's vital fluid is lost. If the mind is steady, then the fluid also remains steady. The fluid is under the control of the mind. Furthermore, if the <term>shukra</term> remains steady, then life remains steady. If the <term>shukra</term> is destroyed, death occurs. Therefore, life is under the control of the <term>shukra</term>. For this reason, one must certainly protect the <term>shukra</term> and the <term>bindu</term> with great effort. || 90 ||

Regarding the verse *A woman in her season...*: In this manner, one should steady the <term>rajas</term> <note>female essence</note> of a woman in her season and one's own <term>bindu</term>. One who knows the practice of **Vajrolī** is he who can draw the <term>rajas</term> and the <term>bindu</term> upward through the organ with great effort. || 91 ||

Now he speaks of **Sahajolī** and **Amarolī**: *Sahajolī and...* These are specific variations of **Vajrolī**. Sah...

<summary>This page explains the vital connection between mental stability and the preservation of sexual energy (Bindu), asserting that the retention of this fluid is the key to longevity. It introduces the practices of Sahajolī and Amarolī as specialized forms of the Vajrolī technique.</summary>
<keywords>Bindu, Shukra, Vajroli, Sahajoli, Amaroli, Mind-Body Connection, Vital Essence, Hatha Yoga, Rajas, Longevity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Vajrolī Mudrā, moving into the specific variations known as Sahajolī and Amarolī, which involve the ritual use of sacred ash following the physical practice.</meta>

->### THIRD INSTRUCTION ###<-

**Root: After the sexual union of Vajrolī, the man and the woman should smear their own limbs. || 93 ||**
**Sitting comfortably and having ceased their activity in an instant, this is called Sahajolī, and should always be trusted by yogis. || 94 ||**
**This auspicious yoga, even though it is joined with enjoyment, bestows liberation. This yoga succeeds for the virtuous, the steady, and those who see the Truth. || 95 ||**
**It succeeds for those free from malice, but not for those who are full of envy. || 96 ||** <note>The numbering in the root text continues the sequence of the practice's benefits.</note>

### || COMMENTARY ||

Regarding the origin of the substance: One should take the beautiful white ash <note>original: *vibhūti*</note> produced from burnt cow dung, place it in water, and having mixed it with the water, apply it as described in the following verse. || 92 || <note>This commentary refers back to the preparation mentioned at the end of the previous page.</note>

Regarding the verse *Vajrolī...*: For the sake of the <term>Vajrolī Mudrā</term> <note>a complex yogic practice involving the control of sexual energy</note>, there is union. Following that, while the man and woman are sitting comfortably in bliss, having ceased the "festival of delight" <note>original: *ratyutsavān* — refers to the completion of the sexual act</note> and abandoned the physical activity, they should perform the smearing of their own limbs. These beautiful limbs include the crown of the head, the forehead, the eyes, the heart, the shoulders, and the arms. || 93 ||

Regarding the verse *Sahajolī...*: This previously mentioned practice is called <term>Sahajolī</term> by the great yogis such as <term>Matsyendra</term> <note>The legendary founding master of the Haṭha Yoga tradition</note> and others. What is its nature? It is always worthy of faith and should be practiced with devotion. This method known as Sahajolī is "auspicious" <note>original: *śubhakarah*</note>, meaning it brings about one's ultimate welfare or highest good. As the lexicons state: "Yoga means a method of binding, meditation, union, or application." What kind of yoga is this? Even though it is "joined with enjoyment" <note>original: *bhoga-yuktaḥ* — referring to physical pleasure</note>, it still "bestows liberation" <note>original: *mukti-daḥ* — grants spiritual freedom or Mokṣa</note>. || 94 ||

Regarding the verse *This yoga...*: This aforementioned yoga belongs to the virtuous <note>original: *puṇyavantāḥ*</note>, those who possess merit and good deeds...

### || VERNACULAR TRANSLATION ||

...Sahajolī and Amarolī. This is because the results of these practices are the same as the results of <term>Vajrolī</term>. For this practice, one burns cow dung until the resulting ash is white and beautiful. One should then mix that ash with water. || 92 ||

Regarding the verse *Vajrolī...*: After performing the union for the sake of Vajrolī, the man and woman—sitting peacefully and having ceased the physical activity of the festival of delight for a moment—should smear the beautiful limbs of the body, such as the head, forehead, eyes, heart, shoulders, and arms. || 93 ||

Regarding the verse *Sahajolī...*: This practice has been called Sahajolī by yogis like Matsyendra and others. It is worthy of being practiced with faith. It creates auspiciousness. And even though this yoga is joined with the company of a woman, it is still the giver of liberation. || 94 ||

Regarding the verse *This yoga...*: This yoga is for the virtuous, for those who possess patience/steadiness, and for those who see the Truth—not for those who look at the faults or virtues of others...

<sig>15</sig>

<summary>This page describes the practice of Sahajolī, a variation of Vajrolī Mudrā where practitioners smear sacred cow-dung ash on specific points of the body after sexual union. It emphasizes that this path leads to spiritual liberation (Mukti) even though it involves physical enjoyment (Bhoga), provided the practitioner is virtuous and free from envy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the variations of the Vajrolī practice, specifically transitioning from Sahajolī to Amarolī. It explains the specific ritual consumption of fluids and the internal purification methods associated with the Kāpālika tradition.</meta>

( 114 )
->### IN THE HATHA YOGA PRADĪPIKĀ ###<-

## Now, Amarolī ##

**Root: Because of the intensity of bile, one should discard the first stream of water; because it lacks essence, the final stream is also discarded. The cool middle stream is consumed. In the doctrine of Khaṇḍa according to the Kāpālika sect, this is known as Amarolī. || 96 ||**

**He who daily drinks the *Amari* <note>original: *amarī* — literally "the immortal," a euphemism for the sacred water or urine</note> and performs the nasal rinse <note>nasya</note>, and correctly practices *Vajrolī*—that is called *Amarolī*. || 97 ||**

->।। COMMENTARY ।।<-

The practice succeeds for virtuous men who are steady and see the Truth—those who perceive the actual reality of things. It succeeds for those who are free from malice, meaning those who are devoid of envy or hatred toward the qualities of others. As the *Amara-kośa* <note>an ancient Sanskrit dictionary</note> states: "Envy <note>original: *matsara*</note> is the hatred of another's qualities." Success comes to such men. However, for those full of envy, it does not succeed. || 95 || <note>The commentator here finishes explaining the final portion of the previous verse before moving to the new topic.</note>

Regarding the explanation of *Amarolī* in verse 96: The phrase "intensity of bile" <note>original: *pittolbaṇatvāt*</note> refers to the state of being excessive or sharp due to bile. Therefore, one should discard the first stream of the "Water of Shiva" <term>Śivāmbu</term> <note>a sacred term for the urine used in this practice</note>. At the time the *Śivāmbu* begins to flow, one should abandon the very first portion of the stream. Similarly, that which is "without essence" <note>original: *niḥsāra*</note> refers to the final portion. One should discard the very last part of the stream because it lacks substance. The "cool middle stream," which is free from the essence of defects like bile, is the one to be "consumed" or thoroughly used. This is the "doctrine of <term>Khaṇḍa</term>" <note>likely referring to a specific teacher or sub-sect leader</note>, which is the preferred method in the <term>Kāpālika</term> <note>a "skull-bearing" ascetic tradition known for radical practices</note> tradition. This is the meaning of the Kāpālika sect of Khaṇḍa. The term *Amarolī* is well-known in this context. || 96 ||

Regarding the verse *Amarolīm...*: That person who daily drinks the *Amari* <note>the Water of Shiva</note> and performs the nasal rinse through the nose— || 97 ||

->।। VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ।।<-

Success comes to those who are free from envy. Those who feel hatred or jealousy upon seeing the qualities or prosperity of others, and who harbor burning resentment, will not achieve success. || 95 ||

Now *Amarolī* is described. Regarding the verse *Pittolbaṇatvāt...*: At the time of the flow of *Śivāmbu*, one should discard the first stream which has an intense quality due to bile and possesses a slight heat. Then, one should discard the final stream which has no further essence. One should constantly consume the middle stream, which is cool and free from the defects of bile and other humors. This practice of the *Kāpālikas*, which is the preferred method of Yoga, is famous by the name *Amarolī*. || 96 ||

Regarding the verse *Amarolīm...*: The man who takes the *Amari*—also called *Amaravāruṇī* <note>original: *amaravāruṇī* — the "wine of the immortals"</note>—into the back of the nostrils...

<summary>This page defines the practice of Amarolī, which involves the selective consumption and nasal intake of "the Water of Shiva" (urine), specifically advocating for the middle stream while discarding the first and last portions. It attributes this practice to the Khaṇḍa-Kāpālika tradition and emphasizes that success requires a mind free from envy.</summary>

<keywords>Amaroli, Shivambu, Kapalika, Vajroli, bile, middle stream, purification, Amari, envy, Khanda-mata</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Amarolī and Vajrolī mudrās, specifically focusing on the ritual application of fluids mixed with ash and the practice of Vajrolī as performed by women.</meta>

( 115 )
->### THIRD INSTRUCTION ###<-

**Root: Through practice, one should mix the lunar nectar <note>original: *cāndrī* — refers to the "lunar" fluid or *amṛta*</note> that has emerged with sacred ash. When one applies this to the upper parts of the body, divine sight <term>divya-dṛṣṭi</term> <note>the ability to see beyond the physical realm</note> is born. || 98 ||**

**By drawing up the male seed <term>bindu</term> through the skillful mastery of practice, if a woman preserves her own fluid <term>rajas</term> by means of *Vajrolī*, she too is a *Yoginī*. || 99 ||**

## ।। COMMENTARY ।।

Regarding the phrase "daily..." [referring to the end of verse 97]: One who daily performs the intake of the *Amari* <note>the sacred fluid</note> through the nose, while practicing *Vajrolī* correctly—as described in the previous verse beginning with "slowly by the male organ"—that is called *Amarolī*. This is the doctrine held by the *Kāpālikas* <note>an ascetic sect of Shiva-worshippers</note>. The term *Amari-pāta* refers to the "falling of the immortal fluid." The meaning is that *Vajrolī* preceded by a nasal rinse <note>original: *nasya*</note> is designated by the term *Amarolī*. || 97 ||

Regarding the verse beginning with "Through practice" [Verse 98]: One should mix the lunar nectar—that which belongs to the moon and has emerged from the practice of *Amarolī*—with sacred ash <note>original: *vibhūti*</note>. One should apply <note>literally "hold"</note> this mixture of ash and lunar fluid to the "upper limbs"—namely the skull, forehead, eyes, shoulders, throat, heart, and arms. Thus, "divine sight" is born in such a person, meaning they possess the "divine eye" capable of seeing objects that are past, future, present, or hidden and distant. The specific methods of using *Amari* are to be understood from the *Śivāmbu-kalpa* <note>a traditional text dedicated to the therapeutic and spiritual use of urine, referred to as "the water of Shiva"</note>. || 98 ||

Having explained the practice of *Vajrolī* for men, the author now speaks of it for women in the verse beginning "male seed" [Verse 99]: Through the "skillful mastery of practice"—meaning the cleverness gained from correct repetition—if a woman draws up the *bindu* <note>the male seed or vital essence</note> and preserves her own *rajas* <note>the female vital fluid or menstrual essence</note> through the *Vajrolī Mudrā*, that woman should be known as a *Yoginī* <note>a female practitioner of Yoga</note> possessing excellent Yoga. In the variant reading "joined with the male seed," the phrase serves as an adjective for the female fluid (*rajas*). || 99 ||

## ।। HINDI TRANSLATION ।।

One who drinks the *Amari* and daily practices *Vajrolī* follows what is called the *Amarolī* of the *Kāpālikas*. || 97 ||

Regarding "Through practice" [Verse 98]: Take the nectar of the moon that has emerged from the practice of *Amarolī*, and mix it with the ash mentioned previously. If one applies this to the "upper limbs"—such as the head, eyes, shoulders, heart, and arms—one’s vision becomes "divine sight," capable of seeing the past, future, and present. || 98 ||

Now, after explaining the *Vajrolī* practice for men, the practice for women is explained [Verse 99]: If a woman, through her skill in practice, draws up the male seed and protects her own female fluid through the *Vajrolī Mudrā*, she should be known as a *Yoginī*—one in whom Yoga is truly present. || 99 ||

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Vajrolī Mudrā, specifically addressing its benefits for women practitioners and the internal alchemical union of male and female vital principles within the body.</meta>

( 116 )
->हठयोगप्रदीपिकायां<-
->**IN THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA**<-

**Root: For her, not even a tiny bit of her vital fluid <term>rajas</term> <note>original: *rajas* — refers to the female reproductive or vital fluid</note> is lost; of this, there is no doubt. In her body, the internal sound <term>nāda</term> <note>the mystical resonance heard in deep meditation</note> transforms into the state of the seed <term>bindu</term>. || 100 ||**

**That male seed <term>bindu</term> and that female fluid <term>rajas</term> become one within one's own body; through the practice of the *Vajrolī* yoga, they grant every spiritual perfection <term>siddhi</term>. || 101 ||**

---

### ।। COMMENTARY ।।

The author describes the result of *Vajrolī* when performed by a woman: "For her..." For that woman who is habituated to the practice of *Vajrolī*, her vital fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note>—not even a tiny, slight amount—goes to waste; meaning, it does not perish or fall away. "Of this, there is no doubt"—there is no uncertainty here. In the body of that woman, the internal sound <note>original: *nāda*</note> attains the state of the seed <note>original: *bindutva*</note>. The sound that has risen from the root center <term>mūlādhāra</term> <note>the energy center at the base of the spine</note> goes to the state of the *bindu* above the heart. This means it becomes one with the *bindu*. As stated in the *Amṛtasiddhi* <note>an early Hatha Yoga text</note>:

> "The seed is said to be the male principle <note>original: *pauruṣaṃ*</note>, and the fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note> is that which arises from the woman. By the external union of these two, the procreation of human beings occurs. ||
> When there is an internal union, then one is sung of as a 'Yogi.' The seed <note>original: *bindu*</note> is said to be of the nature of the moon, and the fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note> is likewise of the nature of the sun. ||
> Only from the meeting of these two is the supreme state <note>original: *paramaṃ padaṃ*</note> produced. This union of the seed is the giver of heaven, the giver of liberation, the giver of merit, and even the giver of demerit. ||
> Within that union, all the deities reside in a subtle form."

|| 100 ||

Regarding the verse "That seed...": That seed <note>original: *bindu*</note> of the man and that fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note> of the woman become one—meaning they are joined together—through the practice of *Vajrolī Mudrā*. Residing thus within one's own body, they grant all perfections. || 101 ||

---

### ।। VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ।।

Now the fruit of the *Vajrolī* performed by a woman is described: "For her..." For that woman whose nature and habit are fixed in the practice of *Vajrolī*, her fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note> does not perish or fall away even in the slightest. There is no doubt in this. In that woman's body, the sound <note>original: *nāda*</note> attains the state of the *bindu*. The sound that rose from the root center <note>original: *mūlādhāra*</note> becomes unified with the *bindu* above the heart. The male seed and the female fluid—through their external union, the creation of humanity happens. But when through practice there is an internal union of the fluid and the seed, then that person is called a Yogi. Furthermore, the seed is moon-like, and the fluid is sun-like. From their meeting, the supreme state is attained. This union of the seed is the giver of heaven, liberation, and merit; likewise, all the deities remain present in a subtle form within the union of the seed and fluid. || 100 ||

"That seed...": The male seed and the female fluid—if these two meet through the practice of *Vajrolī* and are maintained within one's own body, they grant all perfections <note>original: *siddhis*</note>. || 101 ||

<summary>This page explains that Vajrolī Mudrā allows women to preserve their vital essence (rajas), leading to an internal union of male and female principles (bindu and rajas). This alchemical marriage within the practitioner's body is described as the source of spiritual liberation and supernatural powers.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Vajrolī Mudrā, moving from the internal alchemical union of fluids to the specific results for a female practitioner (Yogini). It then transitions into the practice of Shaktichālana (stimulating the Kundalini power).</meta>

( 117 )
### THIRD CHAPTER

**Root: She who preserves her vital fluid <term>rajas</term> <note>original: *rajas* — the female reproductive or vital energy</note> by drawing it upward through contraction is indeed a Yogini. She knows the past and the future, and certainly becomes a "sky-walker" <term>Khecharī</term> <note>original: *khecharī* — literally "moving in the sky," referring to both a specific mudra and the spiritual state of moving through the space of consciousness</note>. || 2 ||**

**She attains perfection of the body through the practice of *Vajrolī*. This Yoga produces merit and grants liberation <term>mukti</term> even if worldly pleasures <term>bhoga</term> have been enjoyed. || 3 ||**

### Now, Stimulating the Power <term>Shakti-chālana</term> ||

**The "Crooked-Bodied" One <note>original: *kuṭilāṅgī*</note>, the "Coiled" One <term>Kundalinī</term>, the "Serpent" <note>original: *bhujangī*</note>, "Power" <term>Shakti</term>, the "Sovereign" <note>original: *īśvarī*</note>, *Kundalī*, and *Arundhatī*—all these words are synonyms. || 4 ||**

->।। COMMENTARY ।।<-

Regarding "She who preserves": The woman who preserves her vital fluid <note>original: *rajas*</note> by drawing it to the upper regions through the contraction of the generative organ—this is well-known in the scriptures of Yoga. Such a woman is a Yogini who knows the past and the future; she knows things that have happened and things yet to come. "Certainly" means it is beyond doubt. She becomes a "sky-walker" <note>original: *khecarī*</note>, meaning she moves through the atmosphere or the inner space of consciousness. || 2 ||

Regarding "Perfection of the body": From the method of the practice of *Vajrolī*, she attains a perfection of the body <note>original: *dehasiddhi*</note> characterized by beauty, charm, strength, and a diamond-like constitution <note>original: *vajrasaṃhanana*</note>. This practice of *Vajrolī* produces merit, generating a specific "unseen" spiritual benefit. What kind of Yoga is it? Even when worldly objects <note>original: *viṣaya*</note> have been enjoyed <note>original: *bhukte 'pi*</note>, it grants liberation or final release. || 3 ||

Wishing to describe the practice of "Stimulating the Power" <note>original: *śakticālana*</note>, the author uses seven verses to introduce it by listing the synonyms of the *Kundalinī* and describing the opening of the door to liberation. Regarding "The Crooked-Bodied One": (1) The Crooked-Bodied One, (2) *Kundalinī*, (3) the Serpent, (4) Power, (5) the Sovereign, (6) *Kundalī*, and (7) *Arundhatī*—these seven words are synonyms, meaning they all refer to the same thing. || 4 ||

->।। VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ।।<-

Regarding "She who preserves": The woman who contracts the vaginal region and carries her vital fluid to the upper centers—in the Yoga scriptures, she is called a Yogini. She knows past and future things. Most certainly, she attains the state of moving through the sky <note>original: *vaimānika gatī* — literally "celestial vehicle movement"</note>. || 2 ||

Regarding "Perfection of the body": By the method of *Vajrolī* practice, what kind of physical perfection is attained? One obtains beauty, charm, strength, and a diamond-hard physical frame. This Yoga of *Vajrolī* practice produces spiritual merit. Furthermore, even if one enjoys the pleasures of the senses, this Yoga still grants liberation. || 3 ||

Now "Stimulating the Power" is described. Regarding "The Crooked-Bodied One": (1) The Crooked-Bodied One, (2) *Kundalinī*, (3) the Serpent, (4) Power, (5) the Sovereign, (6) *Kundalī*, and (7) *Arundhatī*—these seven words all have one and the same meaning. || 4 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Shaktichālana (stimulating the Kundalini power), transitioning from the names of the Goddess to her function as the "key" to liberation and her location within the subtle body.</meta>

( 118 )
# HATHA YOGA PRADĪPIKĀ

**Root: Just as one opens a door with a key by force <term>hatha</term>, so should the Yogi break open the door of liberation <term>moksha</term> using the Kundalinī. || 5 ||**

**The Supreme Goddess <term>Parameśvarī</term> <note>Referring here to Kundalinī</note> lies sleeping, covering with her mouth the entrance to that path by which one must travel to the stainless place of Brahma <note>original: *brahmasthāna* — the state or place of ultimate reality</note>. || 6 ||**

**The Kundalinī power, coiled above the "bulb" <term>kanda</term> <note>original: *kanda* — a bird's-egg-shaped knot at the base of the spine where the psychic channels (nadis) originate</note>, leads to the liberation of Yogis and the bondage of the ignorant. He who knows her knows Yoga. || 7 ||**

->।। COMMENTARY ।।<-

Regarding "Just as": As a person might forcefully open a door with a key—the tool used to move the bolt of a lock—so should the Yogi break open the door of liberation. This "door" refers to the entrance of the central channel <term>Suṣumṇā</term>. The term "forcefully" <note>original: *haṭhāt*</note> is used here according to the "lamp on the threshold" maxim <note>original: *dehalī-dīpika-nyāya* — a logic where a word placed in the middle illuminates both what precedes and what follows</note>; it implies both the physical force used on a door and the specific practice of Hatha Yoga. By breaking through this entrance, one reaches immortality, as the scripture <note>original: *śruti*</note> says: "By that [channel], going upward, one attains immortality." || 5 ||

Regarding "By which": The "stainless place of Brahma" <note>original: *brahmasthāna*</note> is the "Opening of Brahma" <term>Brahmarandhra</term> <note>The fontanel or crown of the head</note>. It is called "stainless" because it is free from the suffering caused by disease and worldly pain. Scripture says: "In the middle of that flame, the Supreme Self is situated." The entrance to the path that must be traveled—the path of the *Suṣumṇā*—is currently blocked. The Supreme Goddess Kundalinī is sleeping there, obstructing the doorway with her mouth. || 6 ||

Regarding "Above the bulb": The Kundalinī power is coiled above the *kanda*. For Yogis, she is the means to liberation when she is awakened; for the ignorant <note>original: *mūḍha* — those who are spiritually "dull" or "deluded"</note>, she remains asleep, keeping them in bondage to the cycle of rebirth. The intent is that Yogis move her and become free, while the ignorant remain bound because they do not know her. One who knows this Kundalinī is a "knower of Yoga" <term>Yogavit</term>. This is because all systems of Yoga are ultimately dependent upon the Kundalinī. || 7 ||

->।। VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ।।<-

Regarding "Just as": In the same way a man opens a door with a key through strength, so too does the Yogi, through the practice of Hatha, pierce the door of liberation—the path of the *Suṣumṇā*—by means of the Kundalinī power. || 5 ||

Regarding "By which": The *Brahmarandhra*, which is free from all suffering, is the destination. The entrance to the path of the *Suṣumṇā* through which one must travel is currently blocked. The Goddess Kundalinī lies sleeping, obstructing the gateway to her own entrance with her mouth. || 6 ||

Regarding "Above the bulb": The Kundalinī power stays above the *kanda*. She is there for the sake of the liberation of Yogis...

<summary>This page uses the metaphor of a key and a lock to describe how Hatha Yoga awakens the Kundalini power to open the central channel (Sushumna). It identifies the Kundalini as the guardian of the path to the Brahmarandhra (the crown of the head), serving as the source of either spiritual liberation or worldly bondage.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the Kundalinī energy, focusing on its physical metaphor as a serpent and its anatomical location within the subtle body, specifically between the psychic channels known as the Ganges and Yamunā.</meta>

->( 119 )<-
->THIRD INSTRUCTION<-

# **Root: The Kundalinī is described as having a coiled form, like a serpent. That person by whom this power is moved is liberated; of this, there is no doubt. || 8 ||**

# **Root: Between the Ganges and the Yamunā dwells the "young widow" <note>original: *bāla-raṇḍā* — a traditional metaphor for Kundalinī, suggesting she is a potent but solitary force awaiting union</note> who is an ascetic. One should seize her by force; this leads to the supreme state of Vishnu <note>original: *viṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam* — the highest state of all-pervading consciousness</note>. || 9 ||**

# **Root: The divine Ganges is the Iḍā channel, and the Yamunā river is the Piṅgalā. Between the Iḍā and the Piṅgalā dwells the "young widow," the Kundalinī. || 10 ||**

### ।। COMMENTARY ।।

Regarding "The Kundalinī": The Kundalinī power is described by Yogis as having a "coiled form" <note>original: *kuṭila-ākāra*</note>, meaning its nature or appearance is like that of a coiled snake. That person by whom this Kundalinī power is "moved"—meaning raised upward from the root center <term>mūlādhāra</term>—is liberated and released from the bonds of ignorance. In this matter, there is no doubt or uncertainty. This is supported by the scripture <note>original: *śruti*</note> which states: "By that [channel], going upward, one attains immortality." || 8 ||

Regarding "Between the Ganges and Yamunā": Because the Iḍā and Piṅgalā channels are meditated upon as being non-different from the Ganges and Yamunā rivers—or because they contain those rivers within them in a meditative sense—they are called "Ganges and Yamunā." Between them, in the path of the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term>, dwells the Kundalinī, who is called the "ascetic" because she remains in a state of fasting <note>i.e., she is dormant or "starved" of prana until awakened</note>. She is also referred to by the term "young widow" <term>bāla-raṇḍā</term>. One should seize her by "force" <term>haṭha</term>. This seizing of her between the Ganges and Yamunā leads one to the supreme state of Vishnu—the all-pervasive Lord or the Supreme Self. || 9 ||

He explains the meaning of the terms Ganges, Yamunā, and so on. Regarding "The Iḍā": The Iḍā is the channel of the left-side breath; it is divine, endowed with lordship and other powers, and is called by the name "Ganges." The Piṅgalā is the channel of the right-side breath, called by the name "Yamunā." || 10 ||

### ।। VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ।।

For the sake of the bondage of the dull-witted, she sleeps; however, the Yogi, by moving this Kundalinī, becomes liberated. Dull people do not know the Kundalinī, and therefore they remain in bondage. He who knows this Kundalinī should be understood as a "knower of Yoga," because all the systems of Yoga <term>yoga-tantra</term> rely upon the Kundalinī. || 7 || <note>The vernacular text here recaps the previous verse before moving to the current ones</note>

Regarding "The Kundalinī": The Kundalinī power is coiled like a serpent. Such is the description of her form given by the Yogis. The man who has moved her—meaning he has caused her to reach upward from the root center <term>mūlādhāra</term>—is said to be liberated. There is no doubt in this. || 8 ||

Regarding "Between the Ganges and Yamunā": Between the Ganges and Yamunā—which are the Iḍā and Piṅgalā—is the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term>. What is she like? She is an ascetic and a "young widow." By using force, one seizes the Kundalinī there. This is what brings one to the supreme state of Vishnu, the all-pervading Soul <term>Hari</term>. || 9 ||

The meaning of the words Ganges and Yamunā is explained. Regarding "The Iḍā": The Iḍā is the channel of the left breath, called the Divine Mother...

# **Root: One should seize the serpent by the tail and awaken her from her sleep. Once she abandons her sleep, that Power (Shakti) rises upward by force. || 11 ||**

# **Root: She resides there, hooded. Every morning and evening, for about half a watch <note>original: *prahara-ardha* — a "prahara" is a three-hour period, so half is 90 minutes or about 4 "ghatika" units</note>, one should daily move her after inhaling through the "Sun" <note>the right nostril</note> according to the "wrapping technique" <term>pari-dhāna-yukti</term>. || 12 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

Regarding "The river": Between the Iḍā and the Piṅgalā channels, located in the middle, is the Kundalinī; she is called the "young widow" <note>original: *bāla-raṇḍā*</note>. || 110 ||

The author now describes the "Stimulation of Power" <term>śakti-cālana</term>. Regarding "By the tail": One should seize the sleeping serpent—the Kundalinī—by the "tail" and awaken her. The meaning is that this Power, the Kundalinī, will then leave her sleep and stand upright by force. Regarding "She resides": She resides facing downward in the root-center <term>mūlādhāra</term>. That hooded serpent is the Kundalinī. After filling the breath through the "Sun" <note>inhaling through the right nostril</note>, and using the "wrapping technique"—the specific method for "putting on the garment"—she should be seized and moved. This should be practiced daily, every morning at sunrise and every evening at sunset, for "half a watch," which means for a duration of two *muhūrtas* <note>about 96 minutes</note>. She is "to be moved," meaning she is fit to be circulated all around. The specific "wrapping technique" <term>pari-dhāna-yukti</term> must be learned from a teacher. || 12 ||

<header>LESSON THREE</header>

### **Root: It is said to be soft, white, and possesses the characteristics of a wrapped cloth. || 13 ||**
### **Root: While in the Thunderbolt Pose <note>Vajrasāna</note>, one should hold the feet firmly with both hands.**
### **Root: One should then firmly press the bulb <term>kanda</term> <note>the energetic center of the body</note> near the region of the ankles. || 14 ||**
### **Root: The Yogi, seated in the Thunderbolt Pose, having moved the Kundalinī...**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

The location is one span <note>original: *vitasti* — about 9 inches</note> above the "root place" <note>the perineum</note>, in the middle between the navel and the genitals. By this, the location of the bulb <term>kanda</term> is described. It is also stated in the *Goraksha Shataka*: "Above the genitals and below the navel is the source of the bulb, shaped like a bird's egg. From there, the seventy-two thousand energy channels <term>nadis</term> originate." 

Yajnavalkya says: "Two fingers above the anus and two fingers below the genitals is the center of the body; this is called the 'body-center' for humans. The location of the bulb for humans is nine fingers from that body-center. It is four fingers in width and of similar length. It is egg-shaped and adorned with skin and other tissues. For four-legged animals, birds, and crawling creatures, it is located in the middle of the belly." 

One finger above the anus, plus one finger for the middle, and then nine fingers to the bulb-location—together this equals twelve fingers, which is the measure of one span. A "collection of four fingers" refers to its four-finger width. Width here also implies length. It is four fingers long, soft, tender, and white (bright). It is described as having the "characteristics of a wrapped cloth" <note>original: *veṣṭitāmbaralakṣaṇam*</note> because its form is like a garment that has been wound or wrapped around. This is how the nature of the bulb is described by the Yogis. || 13 ||

**Regarding "While in":** When the Thunderbolt Pose <note>Vajrasāna</note> is performed, one should hold the feet or the heels firmly with the hands near the ankles—the joints of the feet. Slightly above the ankles. The *Amarakosha* <note>a famous Sanskrit lexicon</note> defines "Gulpha" as the joints of the ankles. One should seize the feet firmly. The word "and" implies that with the feet held firmly, one should strongly press the bulb in its location. The meaning is that after grasping the feet with the hands above the ankles, one should press the bulb in the area below the navel. || 14 ||

**Regarding "In the Thunderbolt Pose":** The Yogi, seated in the Thunderbolt Pose, having moved the Kundalinī via the "Stimulation of Power" gesture <term>śakticālana-mudrā</term>...

->॥ VERNACULAR TRANSLATION ॥<-

Now its nature is described. **Regarding "Upward":** Measuring one span above the "root place," in the middle of the navel and the genitals, is the location of the bulb. In the center of the human body, the bulb-location is nine fingers away. It is four fingers wide, shaped like a bird's egg, soft, and white. Its form is like a wrapped cloth; such is the nature of the bulb as described by Yogis. || 13 ||

**Regarding "While in":** Performing the Thunderbolt Pose, one should catch the feet at the ankles above the heels with the hands, and press the bulb below the navel. || 14 ||

**Regarding "In the Thunderbolt Pose":** The Yogi who is situated in the Thunderbolt Pose, having moved the Kundalinī—that is to say...

<header>IN THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA</header>

**Root: One should subsequently perform the Bellows breath <term>bhastrika</term>; this quickly awakens the Kundalinī. || 15 ||**
**Root: One should contract the Sun <note>the navel center</note> and then move the Kundalinī. For one who does this, even if they have fallen into the mouth of Death, why should they fear death? || 16 ||**
**Root: By moving the energy fearlessly for a period of two *muhurtas* <note>about 96 minutes; one muhurta is roughly 48 minutes</note>, it is drawn slightly upwards into the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term>. || 17 ||**
**Root: By that upward pull, the Kundalinī certainly leaves the entrance of the central channel. Therefore, this breath enters the central channel of its own accord. || 18 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

"Having done so" is the implied meaning. **Subsequently**—that is, after Stimulating the Power <term>śakticālana</term>—one should perform the Bellows, which is the breath retention <term>kumbhaka</term> known as *bhastrika*. In this manner, one should quickly **awaken** the Kundalinī power. Although the Thunderbolt Pose <note>Vajrāsana</note> was prescribed previously for Stimulating the Power, it is mentioned again here to establish the rule that one must remain in the Thunderbolt Pose specifically while performing the Bellows breath after the stimulation. || 15 ||

**Regarding the Sun:** One should perform the contraction of the **Sun**, which is located in the region of the navel. The contraction of the Sun occurs specifically through the contraction of the navel. Then, following the contraction of the Sun, one should move the Kundalinī power. For a man who performs this, even if he has **entered the mouth of Death**—meaning he is facing the end of his life—where is the fear of death? The meaning is that he has no fear of time or death at all. || 16 ||

**Regarding the two muhurtas:** For a **pair of muhurtas**—which consists of four *ghatikas* <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note>—one should move the energy **fearlessly** and without hesitation. By doing so, this power arises within the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term> and is **drawn slightly upwards**. || 17 ||

**Regarding that:** By that upward pull, the Kundalinī [leaves] the **entrance**—the path of entry—of the well-known central channel...

->॥ VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ॥<-

Having performed the Stimulating the Power gesture, one should thereafter perform the breath retention called the Bellows <term>bhastrika</term>. By this method, one should quickly awaken—that is, rouse—the Kundalinī power. || 15 ||

**Regarding the Sun:** One should contract the **Sun** situated in the navel region. By contracting the navel itself, the Sun is contracted. If one moves the Kundalinī power by contracting the Sun, then the person who has reached the mouth of death will have no fear of Time. || 16 ||

**Regarding the two muhurtas:** By moving the energy fearlessly for four *ghatikas*, this power, rising in the central channel, is pulled somewhat upwards. || 17 ||

**Regarding that:** By pulling upwards, the Kundalinī certainly [clears] its own path of entry into the central channel...

<summary>This page details the integration of Bhastrikā (Bellows breath) with Shakticālana to awaken the Kundalinī and force it into the Suṣumṇā channel. It emphasizes that this practice, performed for approximately 96 minutes, overcomes the fear of death by redirecting the life force.</summary>

<header>THE THIRD INSTRUCTION</header>

**Root: Therefore, one should constantly move Arundhatī <note>a traditional name for the Kundalinī energy, literally "the one who does not block"</note> who is sleeping happily. By moving her alone, the yogi is freed from all diseases. || 19 ||**

**Root: The yogi by whom this Power <term>śakti</term> is moved becomes a vessel for perfections <term>siddhis</term>. What is the use of speaking more on this? He conquers Time <note>death</note> with ease. || 20 ||**

**Root: For one devoted to celibacy <term>brahmacharya</term> and who always eats beneficial and moderate food, success is seen within a *mandala* <note>a period of forty days</note> of practicing the yoga of Kundalinī. || 21 ||**

->॥ COMMENTARY ॥<-

On the path, it certainly leaves or abandons the ground. Therefore, because of that departure from the path, this vital breath <term>prāṇa-vāyu</term> moves or goes into the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term> of its own accord. The underlying meaning is that the Kundalinī has already moved out from the opening of the central channel. || 18 ||

**Regarding "Therefore":** Because the breath enters the central channel by moving the Power, one should move or thoroughly stir that Power—the "happily sleeping Arundhatī"—constantly, every single day. By the mere act of moving that Power, the yogi is released or completely freed from diseases such as coughs, asthma, and the frailties of old age. || 19 ||

**Regarding "He by whom":** The yogi by whom the Kundalinī power is moved becomes a vessel or a worthy recipient of the perfections <term>siddhis</term>, such as the power to become infinitesimal <term>aṇimā</term>. In this matter, what is the use of excessive praise or long explanations? The meaning is that there is no need for further talk. He overcomes or defeats Time—which is to say, Death—with ease, as if it were mere play. || 20 ||

**Regarding "Celibacy":** Celibacy <term>brahmacharya</term> is the restraint of the reproductive organs along with the senses like hearing and so on. For one devoted to this...

->॥ VERNACULAR TRANSLATION ॥<-

Abandon the path; by abandoning that path, this vital breath moves into the central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term> by itself. || 18 ||

**Regarding "Therefore":** Therefore, one should daily move Arundhatī—which is the Kundalinī—who is sleeping happily. By the mere act of moving that Power, the yogi is freed from diseases such as cough, asthma, and old age. || 19 ||

**Regarding "He by whom":** The yogi by whom the Kundalinī power is moved becomes a vessel for the perfections like becoming infinitesimal. What is the use of much praise in this? He conquers Death easily—meaning he treats it with disregard. || 20 ||

**Regarding "Celibacy":** Being devoted to restraining the reproductive organs along with the senses like hearing; always eating what is beneficial and wholesome, avoiding a fourth part of the capacity of the stomach; the Kundalinī power...

It emphasizes that these results require a disciplined lifestyle of moderate eating and sensory restraint over a forty-day period.</summary>

<header>ON THE HATHA YOGA PRADĪPIKĀ</header>

**Root: After moving the Kundalinī, one should especially perform the Bhastrā <term>Bellows breath</term>. For the yogi practicing daily in this way, from where can the fear of Death <term>Yama</term> come? || 22 ||**

**Root: In the purification of the seventy-two thousand channels <term>nāḍīs</term>, where is the means of cleansing without the practice of Kundalinī? || 23 ||**

**Root: This central channel <term>suṣumṇā</term>, through firm practice for yogis...**

### ॥ COMMENTARY ॥

For one who always eats beneficial, wholesome, and moderate food—avoiding the final quarter of the stomach’s capacity <note>Hatha Yoga traditionally suggests filling the stomach 2/4 with food, 1/4 with water, and leaving 1/4 empty for the movement of air</note>—the practice of Kundalinī, which is the practice of Stimulating the Power <term>śakticālana</term>, is the very yoga they possess. Following a *maṇḍala* <note>a traditional ritual cycle of forty days</note>, the perfection of breath control <term>prāṇāyāma</term> is seen. 

"From the breath flowing through the right path of the nose, the life-force <term>prāṇa</term> is greatly lengthened. Then, the body becomes filled with nectar like the moon. Having cut through the path-bell <note>original: mārga-ghaṇṭikā; likely referring to the uvula or a subtle gate in the throat</note>, and having entered the channel at the brow-cavity which is subject to the vast fire of Time, he makes that body new again, just like a severed limb that is firmly rejoined <note>original: skandhavat; suggesting a branch or limb that is grafted or restored to health</note>." || 21 ||

**Regarding "After moving":** Having moved the Kundalinī, that is, having performed the Stimulating of the Power; immediately after that, one should perform the breath retention called Bhastrā <term>Bellows</term>. "Daily" means every single day. For the yogi <term>yamin</term> practicing in the manner described, from where can the fear of Yama—the fear of death—arise? It means it cannot come from anywhere. The underlying meaning is that the yogi gains control over their own departure from the body <note>indicating mastery over the time and manner of death</note>. || 22 ||

**Regarding "Seventy-two thousand":** When the task of purifying the impurities within the seventy-two thousand channels <term>nāḍīs</term> is to be done, where is there a means of cleansing without the practice of Kundalinī—without the practice of Stimulating the Power? There is none. The intention is that through the practice of Stimulating the Power alone, the purification of the impurities in all the channels occurs. || 23 ||

**Regarding "This central":** This middle channel, the *suṣumṇā*, for yogis through firm practice of postures such as the *svastikāsana*...

### ॥ VERNACULAR EXPLANATION ॥

The practice of "moving" [Shaktichalana] is itself the Yoga. For such a yogi, after a cycle of forty days <term>maṇḍala</term>, the perfection of breath control <term>prāṇāyāma</term> is visible. || 21 ||

**Regarding "After moving":** After stimulating the Power, one should perform the breath retention called the Bellows <term>bhastrā</term> daily. The yogi who continues practicing in this way has no fear of the King of Death <term>Yamarāja</term>, because the yogi has gained mastery over leaving the body. || 22 ||

**Regarding "Seventy-two thousand":** If one desires to purify the impurities of the seventy-two thousand channels, there is no way to purify them without the practice of Stimulating the Power. Only through the practice of Stimulating the Power are all the channels purified. || 23 ||

**Regarding "This central":** For yogis, through firm practice of postures, breath control, and the Great Seal <term>mahāmudrā</term>, these...
